4.3
Renormalisation

To prove that the formulae (
202
)-(
205
) hold, we must renormalise both sides separately and compare the results. First, are the de Sitter amplitudes renormalisable at all? The de Sitter action (
50
) is the analytic continuation of the AdS action (
25
), which suggests that we can analytically continue the AdS counterterm action to obtain a corresponding dS counterterm action. As we discussed, this suggests that the de Sitter counterterms are of the form (
201
), and we indeed find that such action is sufficient to renormalise the dS amplitudes.

The renormalised dS amplitudes are then defined as

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{a}^{dS}_{i})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[ds^{\text{reg}}(q_{i};\epsilon)+ds^{\text{ct}}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i};\epsilon)\right],

(207)

where (as usual) the counterterm contribution,
ds^{\text{ct}}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})
, depends on the renormalisation scale
\mu
and a set of scheme-dependent constants,
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
.
Equations (
202
)-(
205
) contain imaginary parts of analytically continued renormalised AdS amplitudes on the right-hand side. These are by definition

\displaystyle\im i^{\text{ren}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})

\displaystyle=\im\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[i^{\text{reg}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\epsilon)+i^{\text{ct}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i};\epsilon)\right],

(208)

where
i^{\text{reg}}
is the AdS regulated amplitude and
i^{\text{ct}}
the AdS counterterm contribution (as mentioned earlier, we take without loss of generality
\mu_{dS}=\mu_{AdS}=\mu
).
The AdS counterterms are devised in such a way that the counterterm contribution
i^{\text{ct}}
cancels the divergences of the regulated amplitude,
i^{\text{reg}}
. The question is then whether the scheme-dependence matches or not. As already discussed, there are more counterterms in AdS than in dS, so in general there are more scheme-dependent constants in AdS than in dS. In the following we show that the scheme-dependence of the correlators also matches provided the scheme-dependent constant are mapped to each other via polynomial map,
\mathfrak{b}_{k}=\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i})
.

In order to establish the exact form of the map we must consider how the renormalisation procedures in dS and AdS are related. Essentially, the problem boils down to the question whether the
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
limit and taking the imaginary part commute,

\displaystyle\im\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[i^{\text{reg}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\epsilon)+i^{\text{ct}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i};\epsilon)\right]\overset{?}{=}\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\im i^{\text{reg}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\epsilon)+\im i^{\text{ct}}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i};\epsilon)\right].

(209)

In other words, is the dS counterterm contribution
ds^{\text{ct}}(q_{j};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})
equal to the imaginary part of the analytically continued AdS contribution? In general, the answer is no for the scheme-dependent part, but we find that this part can also be made to agree provided the scheme-dependent constants are connected via a non-trivial map.

In summary, the relation between renormalised amplitudes is

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta\Delta]}(q)=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta\Delta]}(\mathrm{i}q;\mu,\mathfrak{b})},

(210)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})=-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i})}{\prod_{j=1}^{3}\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{j}\Delta_{j}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))},

(211)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}]}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})=-\frac{1}{8}\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))}{\prod_{j=1}^{4}\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{j}\Delta_{j}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})},

(212)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})=\frac{1}{8}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{j}\Delta_{j}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}\left[\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.-\,\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{x}]}(\mathrm{i}q_{1},\mathrm{i}q_{2},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{x}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}]}(\mathrm{i}s,\mathrm{i}q_{3},\mathrm{i}q_{4};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta_{x}\Delta_{x}]}(\mathrm{i}s)}\right],

(213)

where
\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i})
is a map between the scheme-dependent constants that we discuss below.
In the following, we present a derivation of these formulae.

4.3.1
2-point amplitudes

At two points, de Sitter amplitudes are finite for any
\beta>0
and are given by (
3.2.3
). Thus, there is no scheme-dependence for dS 2-point functions.
The AdS amplitudes, on the other hand, become singular at integral, non-negative
\beta
’s. Since their renormalisation requires a counterterm containing two CFT sources and no operator, we are in the case where the dS amplitude must remain finite. It would be convenient, however, to be able to relate the (trivially renormalised) de Sitter amplitude to the renormalised AdS amplitude, so that we can use renormalised expressions on both sides of (
202
).

Let
\beta=n
be a non-negative integer. The renormalised AdS amplitude reads

\displaystyle i^{\text{ren}}_{[n+\frac{d}{2},n+\frac{d}{2}]}(q;\mu,\mathfrak{b})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n}4^{1-n}}{(n-1)!^{2}}q^{2n}\left(-\log\frac{q}{\mu}+\mathfrak{b}\right),

(214)

where
\mu
is the renormalisation scale and
\mathfrak{b}
is a scheme-dependent constant adjustable by a change in
\mu
. By plugging this expression into (
202
) we find that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[n+\frac{d}{2},n+\frac{d}{2}]}(\mathrm{i}q;\mu)}=\frac{4^{n-1}(n-1)!^{2}}{\pi}q^{-2n}=ds^{\text{ren}}_{[n+\frac{d}{2},n+\frac{d}{2}]}(q).

(215)

The renormalisation scale and the scheme-dependent constant drops out and thus for all
\beta>0
, including integral ones, we can write

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta\Delta]}(q)=-\frac{1}{2\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[\Delta\Delta]}(\mathrm{i}q;\mu,\mathfrak{b})}.

(216)

The left-hand side is scheme independent.

4.3.2
Higher-point functions

In this subsection we discuss the higher-point functions. To keep technicalities to a minimum while still discussing all non-trivial issues, we will carry out the renormalisation procedure for a single scalar field of dimension
\Delta=3
. We carry out the procedure directly in de Sitter spacetime and compare it to the AdS result obtained via formulae (
202
)-(
205
). We consider a single massless scalar field,
\varphi
, and the regulated de Sitter action

\displaystyle S_{dS}=-\int\mathrm{d}^{4+2\epsilon}x\sqrt{-g}\left[\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\varphi\partial^{\mu}\varphi+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon(3+\epsilon)\varphi^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{[333]}\varphi^{3}-\frac{1}{24}\lambda_{[3333]}\varphi^{4}\right].

(217)

The 3- and 4-point functions are expressed in terms of amplitudes as

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{3})\rangle\!\rangle=\lambda_{[333]}ds_{[333]}(q_{j})+O(\lambda_{[333]}^{2}),

(218)

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{3})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{q}_{4})\rangle\!\rangle=\lambda_{[3333]}ds_{[3333]}(q_{j})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lambda_{[333]}^{2}\left[ds_{[33;33x3]}(q_{j},s)+(t-\text{channel})+(u-\text{channel})\right]+\ldots

(219)

Looking at tables
1
and
2
,
we see that the amplitudes
ds^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}
and
ds^{\text{reg}}_{[3333]}
are linearly divergent, while
ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}
exhibits double pole. This is the same situation as in AdS case and thus we consider the dS counterterm action inspired by the AdS case,

\displaystyle S^{dS}_{\text{ct}}[\varphi_{(0)},J]=\int\mathrm{d}^{\hat{d}}\bm{x}\left[\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\varphi_{(0)}^{2}J+\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}^{2}\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}+\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{[3333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[3333]}\right)\varphi_{(0)}^{3}J\right],

(220)

where
\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}
,
\mathfrak{r}_{[3333]}
and
\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}
are counterterm constants that we will fix shortly.

Comparison to AdS

The regulated AdS action corresponding to (
220
) reads

\displaystyle S_{AdS}=\int\mathrm{d}^{4+2\epsilon}x\sqrt{g}\left[\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\varphi\partial^{\mu}\varphi-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon(3+\epsilon)\varphi^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{[333]}\varphi^{3}-\frac{1}{24}\lambda_{[3333]}\varphi^{4}\right].

(221)

With this convention, the 3- and 4-point functions of the operator
\mathcal{O}
dual to the AdS field
\varphi
read

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{1})\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{2})\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{3})\rangle\!\rangle=\lambda_{[333]}i_{[333]}(q_{j})+O(\lambda_{[333]}^{2}),

(222)

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{1})\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{2})\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{3})\mathcal{O}(\bm{q}_{4})\rangle\!\rangle=\lambda_{[3333]}i_{[3333]}(q_{j})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda^{2}_{[333]}\left[i_{[33;33x3]}(q_{j},s)+(t-\text{channel})+(u-\text{channel})\right]+\ldots

(223)

where
i_{[333]}
and
i_{[33;33x3]}
can represent both regulated and renormalised AdS amplitudes.

The renormalisation of the theory requires the addition of the boundary counterterm actions. The AdS counterterm action is given by the
Z_{[0]}
part of equation (4.143) (with
Z_{[0]}
given in (4.145)) of
Bzowski:2022rlz
and reads

\displaystyle S^{AdS}_{\text{ct}}[\phi,\mathcal{O}]=\int\mathrm{d}^{3+2\epsilon}\bm{x}\left[\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}\phi^{2}\mathcal{O}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}^{2}\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}+\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{[3333]}\mathfrak{s}_{[3333]}\right)\phi^{3}\mathcal{O}\right],

(224)

where
\phi
is the source for
\mathcal{O}
. The values of the counterterm constants are given by equations (4.30) and (4.77) of
Bzowski:2022rlz
and read

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\Gamma(\epsilon)\mu^{-\epsilon}\left[1+\epsilon\,\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]}+\epsilon^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[333]}+O(\epsilon^{3})\right],

(225)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{[3333]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\mu^{-2\epsilon}\left[1+\epsilon\,\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[3333]}+O(\epsilon^{2})\right],

(226)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{18}\Gamma^{2}(\epsilon)\mu^{-2\epsilon}\left[1+\epsilon\,\left(2\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]}-\tfrac{1}{3}\right)+\epsilon^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[33;33x3]}+O(\epsilon^{3})\right].

(227)

The constants
\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]},\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[333]},\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[3333]}
and
\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[33;33x3]}
parametrise the scheme-dependence. The omitted higher order terms do not contribute to 3- and 4-point functions. It is crucial, however, that the first subleading terms in both terms is related,
i.e.
,
\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[33;33x3]}=2\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]}-\tfrac{1}{3}
.

With the presented counterterm action the renormalised AdS amplitudes become

\displaystyle i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big[i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{i})-\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}\sum_{j=1}^{3}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{j})\Big],

(228)

\displaystyle i^{\text{ren}}_{[3333]}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[3333]})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big[i^{\text{reg}}_{[3333]}(q_{i})+\mathfrak{s}_{[3333]}\sum_{j=1}^{4}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{j})\Big],

(229)

and

\displaystyle i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]},\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[333]},\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[33;33x3]})=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big[i^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\,\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}\left(i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{1},q_{2},s)+i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(s,q_{3},q_{4})\right)+\mathfrak{s}^{2}_{[333]}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(s)+\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}\sum_{j=1}^{4}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{j})\Big].

(230)

We can substitute these to the (naive) holographic formula (
202
)-(
205
) and compare with the actual renormalised dS amplitude. Let us denote such amplitudes by
\overline{ds}^{\,\text{ren}}
:

\displaystyle\overline{ds}^{\,\text{ren}}_{[333]}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})}{\prod_{j=1}^{3}\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})},

(231)

\displaystyle\overline{ds}^{\,\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}\left[\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\,\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{1},\mathrm{i}q_{2},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}s,\mathrm{i}q_{3},\mathrm{i}q_{4};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{i})}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}s)}\right].

(232)

Are these equal to the genuine renormalised de Sitter amplitudes?

3-point function

We include the counterterm action (
220
) in the generating functional (
149
). By taking functional derivatives with respect to
J
we obtain additional insertions of higher powers of
\varphi_{(0)}
, which lead to the renormalised dS correlations functions. As far as the 3-point amplitude goes, we have

\displaystyle\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\rangle_{\text{ren}}=

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\langle\left[\varphi_{(0)}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\varphi_{(0)}^{2}\right](\bm{x}_{1})\left[\varphi_{(0)}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\varphi_{(0)}^{2}\right](\bm{x}_{2})\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[\varphi_{(0)}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\varphi_{(0)}^{2}\right](\bm{x}_{3})\rangle_{\text{reg}}+O(\lambda_{[333]}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\left(\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\left.+\,(\bm{x}_{1}\leftrightarrow\bm{x}_{2})+(\bm{x}_{1}\leftrightarrow\bm{x}_{3})\right)\right]+O(\lambda_{[333]}^{2}).

(233)

After Fourier transforming we obtain,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(q_{i})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[ds^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{i})+\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\left(ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{1})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{2})+\rm cyclic\ in\ q_{1}\to q_{2}\to q_{3}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\frac{\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i})-\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}{\prod_{j=1}^{3}\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}\right].

(234)

The limit of the 2-point functions is finite and thus we can replace them with the renormalised expression
\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})
.
Using that the scaling dimensions
\hat{D}_{[333]}
and
\hat{D}_{[33]}
of the regulated AdS amplitudes
i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}
and
i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}
, respectively, are
\hat{D}_{[333]}=3-\epsilon,\hat{D}_{[33]}=3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i})-\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})\right]=

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\hat{D}_{[333]}\right)i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{i})-\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\hat{D}_{[33]}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{3}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=-\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\left(1-\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{8}+O(\epsilon^{4})\right)i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{i})-\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\sum_{j=1}^{3}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{j})\right].

(235)

Since the regulated amplitude is linearly divergent, the term of order
\epsilon^{2}
is irrelevant when the limit
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
is taken. Thus, if we take
\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}=\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}
the expression reduces to (
228
). On the other hand the renormalised AdS amplitude
i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}
takes form

\displaystyle i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(q_{i};\mu)=f_{0}(q_{i})+f_{1}(q_{i})\log\left(\frac{q_{i}}{\mu}\right),

(236)

where
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
are homogeneous functions of degree
3
. Thus,

\displaystyle\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i};\mu)

\displaystyle=-i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(q_{i};\mu)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left[\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i})-\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})\right]

(237)

and we arrive at the expected equality,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}\left(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{b}_{[333]}^{(1)}\right)=\overline{ds}^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(q_{i};\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{[333]}^{(1)}).

(238)

where we explicitly included how the scheme-dependent constant matches.

Exchange 4-point function

The situation is more complicated for the 4-point function. By including both terms in the counterterm action (
220
), we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{4})\rangle_{\text{ren}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left\{\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{4})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\right.

\displaystyle+\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{4})\rangle_{\text{reg}}+11\text{ perms.}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{[333]}^{2}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}^{2}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{4})\rangle_{\text{reg}}+11\text{ perms.}

\displaystyle\left.+\,3\lambda_{[333]}^{2}\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{2})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{3})\rangle_{\text{reg}}\langle\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{1})\varphi_{(0)}(\bm{x}_{4})\rangle_{\text{reg}}+3\text{ perms.}\right\}

\displaystyle+O(\lambda_{[333]}^{3}).

(239)

We keep terms of order
\lambda_{[333]}^{2}
, split all the terms into those corresponding to
s
-,
t
- and
u
-channels, and Fourier transform to momentum space. This yields the renormalised dS amplitude

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left\{ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\left[ds^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{3},q_{4},s)\left(ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{1})+ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{2})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\left.+ds^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{1},q_{2},s)\left(ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{3})+ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{4})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}^{2}ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(s)\left[ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{1})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{3})+ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{1})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{4})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\left.+ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{2})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{3})+ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{2})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{4})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\,\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}\left[ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{1})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{2})ds^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{3})+3\text{ perms.}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big\{\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s)+\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}\sum_{j=1}^{4}\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}s)}\left[\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{1},\mathrm{i}q_{2},\mathrm{i}s)-\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\left(\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{1})+\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{2})\right)\right]\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\times\left[\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}s,\mathrm{i}q_{3},\mathrm{i}q_{4})-\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}\left(\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{3})+\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{4})\right)\right]\right\}.

(240)

The terms in the square brackets lack one term to be re-composed into (
228
). With the value of
\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}
already fixed by the renormalisation of the 3-point function and equal to
\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}
in (
225
), we can rewrite this expression as

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})=-\frac{1}{8}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}\left\{A(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad+\,\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big[\im B_{\epsilon}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))+\left(\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}^{2}-\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}+\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{4}\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})\Big]\Big\},

(241)

where

\displaystyle A(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{1},\mathrm{i}q_{2},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}s,\mathrm{i}q_{3},\mathrm{i}q_{4};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})}{\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}s)}

(242)

and
B_{\epsilon}
denotes the combination in (
4.3
) entering the renormalisation of the AdS 4-point amplitude,

\displaystyle B_{\epsilon}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))

\displaystyle=i^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s)-\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}\left(i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{1},q_{2},s)+i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(s,q_{3},q_{4})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathfrak{s}^{2}_{[333]}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(s)+\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}\sum_{j=1}^{4}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{j})

(243)

so that
i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}B_{\epsilon}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})
. At this point, the dependence between the dS counterterm constants
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
and the AdS constants
\mathfrak{b}_{i}
is not yet fixed, with the exception of
\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{b}_{[333]}^{(1)}
. The imaginary parts lead to factors of
\sin(\pi\hat{D}/2)
, where the regulated dimensions of the amplitudes under consideration are

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{[33;33x3]}

\displaystyle=3-2\epsilon,

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=3-\epsilon,

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{[33]}

\displaystyle=3.

(244)

Thus, the subleading terms in the expansion of the sines do contribute. Indeed, we find

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\im B_{\epsilon}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})

\displaystyle=-\Big[\left(1-\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{2}\right)i^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\,\left(1-\frac{\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{8}\right)\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}\left(i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(q_{1},q_{2},s)+i^{\text{reg}}_{[333]}(s,q_{3},q_{4})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathfrak{s}^{2}_{[333]}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(s)+\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}\sum_{j=1}^{4}i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(q_{j})+O(\epsilon)\Big]

\displaystyle=-i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})+\frac{\pi^{2}}{72}\left(q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3}+q_{3}^{3}+q_{4}^{3}+2s^{2}\right).

(245)

On the other hand the renormalised AdS amplitude,
i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}
, contains square of the logarithm of the renormalisation scale,
\log^{2}\mu
. This means that under the analytic continuation we find

\displaystyle\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})=-i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})+\frac{\pi^{2}}{72}\left(q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3}+q_{3}^{3}+q_{4}^{3}+2s^{2}\right).

(246)

Thus, when put together,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\im B_{\epsilon}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\im\left[i^{\text{reg}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s)+i^{\text{ct}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}).

(247)

Substituting back to (
4.3
), we end up with

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(q_{i},s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{i})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}q_{j})}\Big[\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}(\mathrm{i}q_{i},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{b}_{k}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}q_{1},\mathrm{i}q_{2},\mathrm{i}s;\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})\im i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}(\mathrm{i}s,\mathrm{i}q_{3},\mathrm{i}q_{4};\mu,\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})}{\im i^{\text{reg}}_{[33]}(\mathrm{i}s)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}^{2}-\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}+\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{4}q_{j}^{3}\Big].

(248)

In order to match our desired formula (
213
), we must cancel the last term,
i.e.
, the dS renormalisation constant
\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}
is not equal to
\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}
, but rather

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}=\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}-\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}^{2}+O(\epsilon).

(249)

Thus the scheme-dependent constants between the dS and AdS expressions are non-trivially related.

In total we find is that the scheme-dependence matches between dS and AdS for contact diagrams, since

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}(\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}(\mathfrak{b}_{[333]}^{(1)}),

\displaystyle\text{with }\mathfrak{b}_{[333]}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)},

(250)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{[3333]}(\mathfrak{a}_{[3333]}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{s}_{[3333]}(\mathfrak{b}_{[3333]}^{(1)}),

\displaystyle\text{with }\mathfrak{b}_{[3333]}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{a}_{[3333]}^{(1)},

(251)

where the AdS constants
\mathfrak{s}_{[333]}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{[3333]}
are given by (
225
) and (
226
). For the exchange diagram we have
\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}=\mathfrak{s}_{[33;33x3]}
, provided that the relation between dS and AdS scheme-dependent constants reads

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{a}^{(2)}_{[333]},\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{a}^{(1)}_{[33;33x3]}=2\mathfrak{a}^{(1)}_{[333]}+\frac{1}{3}=2\mathfrak{b}^{(1)}_{[333]}+\frac{1}{3},

(252)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{(2)}_{[33;33x3]}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{a}^{(2)}_{[33;33x3]}+2(\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)})^{2}+4\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(2)}.

(253)

4.3.3
Concluding remarks

The analysis carried out here concerns only the 3- and 4-point renormalised amplitudes
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}
,
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[3333]}
and
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33;33x3]}
. We have further analysed all the remaining 3- and 4-point functions under consideration to show that the formulae (
210
)-(
213
) hold in all cases. We evaluated explicitly the constants
a^{[0]}_{1}
and
a^{[0]}_{2}
from equation (
15
) in the theory described by the action (
217
). In the beta scheme, we found

\displaystyle a^{[0]}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[333]},

(254)

\displaystyle a^{[0]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{[333]}^{2}\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}+\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{[3333]}\mathfrak{r}_{[3333]},

(255)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\Gamma(\epsilon)\mu^{-\epsilon}\left[1+\epsilon\,\mathfrak{a}^{(1)}_{[333]}+\epsilon^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{(2)}_{[333]}+O(\epsilon^{3})\right],

(256)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{[3333]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\mu^{-2\epsilon}\left[1+\epsilon\,\mathfrak{a}^{(1)}_{[3333]}+O(\epsilon^{2})\right],

(257)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{[33;33x3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{18}\Gamma^{2}(\epsilon)\mu^{-2\epsilon}\left[1+\epsilon\,\left(2\mathfrak{a}^{(1)}_{[333]}+\tfrac{1}{3}\right)+\epsilon^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{(2)}_{[33;33x3]}+O(\epsilon^{3})\right].

(258)

4.4
Results

Here we list all renormalised de Sitter amplitudes. In all cases, (
210
)-(
213
) are satisfied.

4.4.1
Preliminaries

Consider a real homogeneous function
f=f(\bm{q})
of some momenta
\bm{q}
. Let
D
be the homogeneity degree of
f
(
e.g.
,
f=q^{D}
has the degree equal to
D
.) Then

\displaystyle\im f(\mathrm{i}q)=f(q)\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right).

(259)

With
D
being an integer, this expression vanishes for even
D
’s and equals
\pm f(q)
for odd
D
’s. For integral
D
’s we have

\displaystyle\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{ if }D\text{ is even},\\
+1&\text{ if }D\equiv 1\text{ mod }4,\\
-1&\text{ if }D\equiv 3\text{ mod }4,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{ if }D\text{ is odd},\\
+1&\text{ if }D\equiv 0\text{ mod }4,\\
-1&\text{ if }D\equiv 2\text{ mod }4.\end{array}\right.

The homogeneity degree
D
of the
n
-point AdS amplitude of operators of dimensions
\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{n}
reads

\displaystyle D=\Delta_{t}-(n-1)d,

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Delta_{j}.

(266)

Only if the amplitude is divergence free, and thus no renormalisation is required, is it represented by a truly homogeneous function of degree
D
. Otherwise, renormalisation introduces scale-violating logarithms of the form
\log(q/\mu)
, where
\mu
is the renormalisation scale. In such cases, the scale-violating logarithms and their powers multiply homogeneous functions of degree
D
.

By placing the branch cuts of logarithms on the negative real axis we have

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{i}q}{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{q}{\mu}\right)+\frac{\mathrm{i}\pi}{2}.

(267)

Thus, depending on the homogeneity degree
D
such logarithms may or may not survive the analytic continuation. Indeed, for AdS amplitudes with single logarithms, the dS amplitude will be scale-free if the naive degree is even. The precise form of the dS amplitude depends on the degree of divergence of the regulated AdS amplitude. Since the AdS amplitudes in this paper exhibit at most a double pole in the regulator, we consider three cases as follows.

No divergence.

The AdS amplitude is scale-independent,
f=f(\bm{q})
and we have

\displaystyle\im f(\mathrm{i}q)=f(q)\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right),

(268)

where
D
is given by (
266
) and the sine simplifies according to (
4.4.1
).

Linear divergence.

If the regulated AdS amplitude is linearly divergent, then it can be written in the form

\displaystyle f(\bm{q}_{j};\mu)=f_{0}(\bm{q}_{j})+f_{1}(\bm{q}_{j})\log\left(\frac{q_{t}}{\mu}\right),

(269)

where
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
are homogeneous functions of degree
D
. It follows that

\displaystyle\im f(\mathrm{i}\bm{q};\mu)=f(\bm{q};\mu)\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right)+\frac{\pi}{2}\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right)f_{1}(\bm{q}).

(270)

The sines and cosines can be simplified according to (
4.4.1
).

Double pole.

Similarly, if the regulated amplitude exhibits a double pole, the renormalised amplitude takes form

\displaystyle f(\bm{q}_{j};\mu)=f_{0}(\bm{q}_{j})+f_{1}(\bm{q}_{j})\log\left(\frac{q_{t}}{\mu}\right)+f_{2}(\bm{q}_{j})\log^{2}\left(\frac{q_{t}}{\mu}\right),

(271)

where
f_{0},f_{1},f_{2}
are homogeneous functions of degree
D
. In such a case

\displaystyle\im f(\mathrm{i}\bm{q};\mu)

\displaystyle=\sin\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right)\left[f(\bm{q};\mu)-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}f_{2}(\bm{q})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\pi}{2}\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2}D\right)\left[f_{1}(\bm{q})+2f_{2}(\bm{q})\log\left(\frac{q_{t}}{\mu}\right)\right].

(272)

4.4.2
2-point amplitudes

The 2-point dS amplitudes read

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[22]}=\frac{1}{2q},\qquad\qquad ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33]}=\frac{1}{2q^{3}}.

(273)

4.4.3
3-point amplitudes

All regulated AdS 3-point amplitudes are linearly divergent and thus each renormalised AdS amplitude contains a scale-violating logarithm containing the renormalisation scale
\mu
. Whether the dS amplitude contains a scale-violating logarithm depends on the dimensions
\Delta_{j}
. According to (
270
) and (
4.4.1
) the logarithm drops from
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[222]}
and
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[332]}
, while remains present in
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[322]}
and
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}
. To be precise,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}},

(274)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[322]}}{4q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}}\left\{-q_{1}+(q_{2}+q_{3})\left[\log\left(\frac{q_{t}}{\mu}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}-1\right]\right\},

(275)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{16}\frac{q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}-q_{3}^{2}}{q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}},

(276)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[333]}}{4q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{12q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}}\left\{q_{1}^{2}q_{2}+q_{2}^{2}q_{1}+q_{1}^{2}q_{3}+q_{3}^{2}q_{1}+q_{2}^{2}q_{3}+q_{3}^{2}q_{2}-q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\,(q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3}+q_{3}^{3})\left[\log\left(\frac{q_{t}}{\mu}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}-\frac{4}{3}\right]\right\}.

(277)

4.4.4
4-point contact amplitudes

Here the situation is analogous to the 3-point functions. Only in three amplitudes the AdS 4-point contact diagram contributes non-trivially to the non-local part after analytic continuation:

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[2222]}

\displaystyle=\frac{i_{[2222]}}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}=\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}q_{T}},

(278)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[3322]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[3322]}}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}}\left\{(q_{3}+q_{4})\left[\log\left(\frac{q_{T}}{\mu}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{a}_{[3322]}^{(1)}\right]+\frac{q_{1}q_{2}}{q_{T}}-q_{T}\right\},

(279)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[3333]}

\displaystyle=\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[3333]}}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}}\left\{\frac{1}{3}(q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3}+q_{3}^{3}+q_{4}^{3})\left[\log\left(\frac{q_{T}}{\mu}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{a}_{[3333]}^{(1)}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-\frac{\sigma_{(4)1234}}{q_{T}}+q_{T}\sigma_{(2)1234}-\frac{4}{9}q_{T}^{3}\right\},

(280)

where
\sigma_{(k)1234}
is the
k
-th symmetric polynomial on
q_{1},q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}
, see Appendix
A
. In the remaining two amplitudes the imaginary part of the AdS 4-point contact amplitude is local and we get,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[3222]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{16q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}},

(281)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[3332]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{32}\frac{q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}+q_{3}^{2}-q_{4}^{2}}{q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}}.

(282)

All analytic continuations have been performed in these formulae. For example
i_{[2222]}
in (
278
) is the standard AdS amplitude
i_{[2222]}=i_{[2222]}(q_{i})=1/q_{T}
.

4.4.5
4-point exchange amplitudes

From (
266
) and (
4.4.1
) we see that if the total dimension of the external operators,
\Delta_{T}=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4}
, is odd, the finite part containing the dilogarithm vanishes from the de Sitter amplitude. In such cases only logarithms and scheme-dependent terms survive. For the remaining cases the sine equals
\pm 1
, see table
3
. The dimension of the exchange operator is irrelevant.

Amplitude

\bm{\sin(\frac{\pi D}{2})}

22,22
and
33,33

-1

33,22
and
32,32

+1

32,22
and
32,33

0

Table 3
:
Values of the sine present in (
4.4.1
) for various amplitudes.

There is no more analytic continuation in the formulae below,
i.e.
, the AdS amplitudes on the right-hand sides do not involve analytically continued momenta.
The definitions of the symbols used in the expressions below are presented in appendix
A
.

Exchange dimension
\bm{\Delta_{x}=2}
.

With the exchange scalar of dimension
\Delta_{x}=2
we have the following amplitudes,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[22,22x2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}\left[-i^{\text{ren}}_{[22,22x2]}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{4s}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}\left.i^{\text{ren}}_{[22,22x2]}\right|_{\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}s}\left[\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}\right].

(283)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33,22x2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}}\left[i^{\text{ren}}_{[33,22x2]}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{8s}(q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}-s^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}}\left.i^{\text{ren}}_{[33,22x2]}\right|_{\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}}\left\{\frac{q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}-s^{2}}{2s}\left(\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}\right)+(q_{1}+q_{2})\left[\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{q_{T}}\right)+1\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-\,(q_{3}+q_{4})\left[\log\left(\frac{q_{T}}{\mu}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{a}_{[33,22x2]}^{(1)}-\frac{7}{4}\right]\right\}.

(284)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33,33x2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}}\left[-i^{\text{ren}}_{[33,33x2]}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{16s}(q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}-s^{2})(q_{3}^{2}+q_{4}^{2}-s^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}}\left.i^{\text{ren}}_{[33,33x2]}\right|_{\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}}.

(285)

Since these amplitudes are related to each other by the raising/lowering operators, they share the same structure. As we can see these three dS amplitudes can be obtained from their AdS counterparts by the substitution
\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}
. Not all amplitudes with even
\Delta_{T}
share this feature, though. In particular, we have the following amplitude,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[32,32x2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[32,32x2]}}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}}+\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}s}\left\{\left[(s+q_{2})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)-q_{1}\right]\right.\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[(s+q_{4})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)-q_{3}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\,\frac{\pi^{2}}{8}s(2s+q_{2}+q_{4})\right\}.

(286)

Finally, in two amplitudes with odd
\Delta_{T}
the analytic continuation of the AdS exchange diagrams is local and the non-trivial part of the amplitudes originates from the product of 3-point functions in (
213
),

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[32,22x2]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{16q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}s}\left\{q_{2}\left[\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right]+s\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{l_{34+}}\right)-q_{1}\right\},

(287)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[32,33x2]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{32q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}s}\left\{(q_{3}^{2}+q_{4}^{2}-s^{2})\left[q_{2}\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)+s\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{l_{34+}}\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+s^{2}(q_{1}+q_{2}-q_{3}-q_{4})+\frac{s}{2}\left[-q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}+(q_{3}+q_{4})^{2}\right]-(q_{1}+q_{2})(q_{3}^{2}+q_{4}^{2})\right\},

(288)

Exchange dimension
\bm{\Delta_{x}=3}
.

For the exchange
\Delta_{x}=3
scalar expressions are more complicated. The only dS amplitude, which can be obtained from the corresponding AdS amplitude by the substitution
\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}
is

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[32,32x3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}}\left[i^{\text{ren}}_{[32,32x3]}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{16s^{3}}(s^{2}+q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2})(s^{2}+q_{3}^{2}-q_{4}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}}\left.i^{\text{ren}}_{[32,32x3]}\right|_{\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}}.

(289)

The remaining amplitudes of even total degree
\Delta_{T}
do not exhibit this feature and become more complicated,

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[22,22x3]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[22,22x3]}}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}+\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}s^{3}}\left\{(q_{1}+q_{2})\left[\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right]-s\right\}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left\{(q_{3}+q_{4})\left[\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right]-s\right\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}\left\{\frac{(q_{1}+q_{2})(q_{3}+q_{4})}{2s^{3}}\left[\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-2\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\,\frac{q_{T}}{s^{2}}\left(\log\left(\frac{q_{T}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)\right\}.

(290)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33,22x3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[33,22x3]}}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}}+\frac{1}{24q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}q_{4}s^{3}}\left\{\left[(s^{3}+q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-\frac{4}{3}+\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-\sigma_{(1)12s}\sigma_{(2)12s}+4\sigma_{(3)12s}\right]\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\times\left[(q_{3}+q_{4})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)-s\right]+\frac{\pi^{2}}{8}s^{3}(q_{3}+q_{4})\right\}.

(291)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[33,33x3]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i^{\text{ren}}_{[33,33x3]}}{8q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}}+\frac{1}{72q_{1}^{3}q_{2}^{3}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}s^{3}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left\{\frac{1}{s^{3}}\left[(s^{3}+q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-\frac{4}{3}+\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}\right)-\sigma_{(1)12s}\sigma_{(2)12s}+4\sigma_{(3)12s}\right]\times\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[(s^{3}+q_{3}^{3}+q_{4}^{3})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-\frac{4}{3}+\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}\right)-\sigma_{(1)s34}\sigma_{(2)s34}+4\sigma_{(3)s34}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{\pi^{2}}{8}\left(q_{1}^{3}+q_{2}^{3}+q_{3}^{3}+q_{4}^{3}+2s^{3}\right)\right\}.

(292)

The remaining amplitudes of odd total dimension
\Delta_{T}
receive non-trivial contribution only from the product of the 3-point function in (
213
),

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[32,22x3]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{32q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}s^{3}}\left\{(s^{2}+q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2})\left[(q_{3}+q_{4})\left(\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-1+\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}\right)-s\right]+s^{3}\right\},

(293)

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[32,33x3]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{96q_{1}^{3}q_{2}q_{3}^{3}q_{4}^{3}}\left\{\frac{1}{s^{3}}(s^{2}+q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2})(s^{3}+q_{3}^{3}+q_{4}^{3})\left[\log\left(\frac{l_{34+}}{\mu}\right)-\frac{4}{3}+\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-(s^{2}+q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2})\left[\log\left(\frac{l_{12+}}{\mu}\right)-\frac{4}{3}+\mathfrak{a}_{[333]}^{(1)}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+s(q_{1}-q_{2}-q_{3}-q_{4})-\frac{1}{2}q_{T}(q_{1}+q_{2}-q_{3}-q_{4})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\,\frac{(q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2}+q_{3}q_{4})(q_{3}+q_{4})}{s}-\frac{(q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2})(q_{3}^{2}-q_{3}q_{4}+q_{4}^{2})}{s^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-\,\frac{(q_{1}^{2}-q_{2}^{2})q_{3}q_{4}(q_{3}+q_{4})}{s^{3}}\right\}.

(294)

4.5
Comparison to the literature

Relatively few exact expressions for de Sitter amplitudes are available in the existing literature.
As far as exchange 4-point functions are concerned, only the finite amplitude
ds_{[22,22x2]}
is abundantly present: see, for example, equations
(5.75) of
Arkani-Hamed:2015bza
; (4.54) of
Arkani-Hamed:2018kmz
; (4.52) of
Sleight:2019mgd
; (4.84) of
Sleight:2019hfp
; (3.41) of
Baumann:2021fxj
. All these expressions match (
4.4.5
).

To our knowledge, the only other de Sitter exchange 4-point amplitude with non-derivative vertices to have been calculated previously is
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[22;22x3]}
.
This amplitude was first calculated in equation (4.90) of
Sleight:2019hfp
,
however the result obtained disagrees with ours here.
This is not unexpected given the regulated amplitude
ds^{\text{reg}}_{[22;22x3]}
is divergent and requires renormalisation. Indeed, our result (
4.4.5
) contains the renormalisation scale
\mu
and a scheme-dependent constant,
\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}
, while equation (4.90) of
Sleight:2019hfp
does not.
Our result is therefore anomalous under scale transformations while that of
Sleight:2019hfp
transforms homogeneously.
To see the mismatch more clearly, let
ds_{[22;22x3]}|_{*}
denote (4.90) of
Sleight:2019hfp
. It is easy to check that, in our conventions, it reads

\displaystyle ds_{[22;22x3]}\Big|_{*}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}\left.i^{\text{ren}}_{[22;22x3]}\right|_{\mathcal{D}^{(+)}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{(+)}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}}\left[i^{\text{ren}}_{[22;22x3]}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}\frac{(q_{1}+q_{2})(q_{3}+q_{4})}{s^{3}}\right]

(295)

where we fixed the overall normalisation constant to match our conventions. The resulting expression is scale independent and misses the terms proportional to the product of the 3-point functions in (
205
). By comparing with (
4.4.5
), we see that the difference is

\displaystyle ds^{\text{ren}}_{[22;22x3]}-ds_{[22;22x3]}\Big|_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}q_{4}s^{3}}\left[i^{\text{ren}}_{[223]}(q_{1},q_{2},s;\mu)i^{\text{ren}}_{[322]}(s,q_{3},q_{4};\mu)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\,\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}(q_{1}+q_{2})(q_{3}+q_{4})\right].

(296)

More recently, this same amplitude
ds^{\text{ren}}_{[22;22x3]}
was recomputed through bootstrap considerations in section 3.3 of
Wang:2022eop
, see equations (3.35), (3.41), (3.42) and (3.45).
After accounting for differences in notation and the overall normalisation, our result (
4.4.5
) agrees with that of
Wang:2022eop
upon setting the scheme-dependent constant

\mathfrak{a}_{[322]}^{(1)}=\gamma_{E}+\log(-\tau_{0}\mu)

(297)

where
\tau_{0}
(denoted
\eta_{0}
in
Wang:2022eop
) is the late-time cut-off in conformal time.

4.5.1
Shift operators and derivative vertices

In
Arkani-Hamed:2018kmz
, a family of shift operators
\mathcal{W}_{ij}^{\pm\pm}
were introduced. When acting on a given AdS Witten diagram, these shift operators increase or decrease the value of the external dimensions
\Delta_{i}
and
\Delta_{j}
by one. The resulting expressions, however, represent Witten diagrams where the interactions contain additional derivatives acting on the fields of the form
\varphi(\partial\varphi)^{2}

Bzowski:2022rlz
.
14
14

14

For AdS shift operators that map contact diagrams to contact diagrams, and exchange diagrams to exchange diagrams
without
introducing derivative vertices, see
Caloro:2023cep
.
In the presence of such interactions, the propagators (bulk-to-boundary and bulk-to-bulk) may contain derivatives acting on them. In this subsection we make a few comments about such amplitudes – for a detailed analysis, see
Bzowski:2023jwt
.

Let us consider tree-level exchange diagrams based on an action with the only derivative interaction being
\varphi_{i}(\partial\varphi_{[3]})^{2}
, where
\varphi_{[3]}
is the bulk field dual to a dimension 3 operator and
\varphi_{i}
is either
\varphi_{[3]}
or
\varphi_{[2]}
, the bulk field dual to a dimension 2 operator, plus the standard non-derivative cubic interactions involving fields dual to operators of dimension 2 and 3. One may distinguish the new exchange diagrams constructed using the derivative interactions from a standard exchange diagrams based on non-derivative interactions by indicating which propagator has a derivative acting on it. We will do this by putting a hat to indicate which propagator contains a derivative.
For example,
i_{[\hat{3}\hat{3},33x3]}
is constructed using one derivative vertex
\varphi_{[3]}(\partial\varphi_{[3]})^{2}
and one non-derivative vertex,
\varphi_{[3]}^{3}
and the derivatives act on two bulk-to-boundary propagator;
i_{[\hat{3}2,\hat{3}2x\hat{3}]}
indicates the exchange diagram constructed using two derivative vertices
\varphi_{[2]}(\partial\varphi_{[3]})^{2}
, and each of the bulk-to-boundary propagators of
\varphi_{[3]}
has a derivative acting on it, and two derivatives act on the bulk-to-bulk propagator.

Consider the AdS exchange diagram
i_{[\hat{3}\hat{3},\hat{3}\hat{3}x3]}
for a massless scalar.
As discussed in section 6.3.2 of
Bzowski:2022rlz
, this diagram is connected with
i_{[22,22x3]}
via the action of
weigh-shifting operator:

i_{[\hat{3}\hat{3},\hat{3}\hat{3}x3]}=\mathcal{W}_{12}^{++}\mathcal{W}_{34}^{++}i_{[22,22x3]}.

(298)

The integral
i_{[22,22x3]}
is finite and hence the right-hand side of (
298
) is also finite. More non-trivially, we find that the dilogarithms present in
i_{[22,22x3]}
all cancel out after repeated application of the standard dilogarithm identities leaving
i_{[\hat{3}\hat{3},\hat{3}\hat{3}x3]}
a rational function. The result is

\displaystyle i^{\text{reg}}_{[\hat{3}\hat{3},\hat{3}\hat{3}x3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{3}}{4}+\frac{s^{2}}{4}\left[\frac{2\sigma_{4}}{q_{T}^{3}}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{q_{T}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{2}}{q_{T}}-q_{T}\right]+\frac{\tau\sigma_{4}}{2q_{T}^{3}}+\frac{\tau\sigma_{3}}{4q_{T}^{2}}+\frac{-\tau^{2}+2\tau\sigma_{2}+2\sigma_{4}}{4q_{T}}-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}.

(299)

where
\sigma_{k}=\sigma_{(k)1234}
is the
k
-th symmetric polynomial on
q_{1},q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}
and

\displaystyle\tau=(q_{1}+q_{2})(q_{3}+q_{4}).

(300)

The five polynomials,
\sigma_{k}
for
k=1,2,3,4
and
\tau
form the basis of minimal dimension of the polynomials invariant under
q_{1}\leftrightarrow q_{2}
,
q_{3}\leftrightarrow q_{4}
as well as simultaneous
(q_{1},q_{2})\leftrightarrow(q_{3},q_{4})
.

Amplitude
